L1183: ## Method
L1184: ## NDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 210923}
## {MULPLETA® Lusutrombopag}
L1185: The FDA reviewer explored the applicant's final population PK and PK/PD models by evaluating the diagnostic plots. Using applicant's final population PK/PD model, the reviewer conducted Monte Carlo simulation to assess the effect of body weight and/or baseline platelet count on the response. CDC dataset was used to get the natural distribution of influential covariates, where patients weighting more than 100 kg constituted 16.6%. The FDA reviewer defined a responder as a patient who would achieve platelet count of at least 5 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} in all days from day 9 to day 14. The target population were Caucasians with Child-Pugh score less than 9. Impact on platelet count by increasing the dose from 3-mg to 4-mg was evaluated.
L1186: ## Results
L1187: The applicant's final model was repeatable. According to FDA reviewer's evaluation, the diagnostic plots of the applicant's final PPK model showed that the model adequately described the PK of lusutrombopag (Figure 20). The FDA reviewer also conducted a sensitivity analysis by estimating the exponent of body weight (incorporated through allometric scaling) on clearance. The estimate of the exponent was 0.76, which was comparable to 0.75, and the estimate of clearance remained the same. In addition, the drop in objective function after estimating the exponent was 1.13. Hence, the reviewer concluded that 0.75 is a valid allometric scaling factor for lusutrombopag PK on total body weight.
L1188: The final PK/PD model developed by the applicant was also repeated and similar parameter estimates were obtained as in Appears this way on original
L1189: Table 8. The diagnostic plots are shown in Figure 21.
L1190: ## Evaluation of body weight effect (Phase 3 data)
L1191: The effect of body weight on PK and platelet count was evaluated following 3-mg lusutrombopag dose. In addition, a virtual population of patients weighting more 100 kg and
L1192: dosed with 4-mg was created. The corresponding AUC was obtained through scaling by assuming linear PK. Using data from Phase 3 studies, the plot of lusutrombopag exposure against body weight is shown in Figure 22. The AUCs of patients who developed drug related thrombosis are shown as red-squares. Based on this observed data, it showed that the drug exposure in the over-weighted patients receiving 4-mg could be well covered by the exposure range observed in the study.
L1193: ## Monte Carlo simulations
L1194: The results of simulations assuming differences in exposure is because of body weight is shown in Figure 23. Clearly there is benefit in increasing the dose to 4-mg in patients weighting more than 100 kg. Interestingly, switching to 4-mg dose results in approximately 10% more patients would maintain platelet count above 5 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} as shown in Figure 23.
L1195: In addition, as shown in Figure 24, there is limited efficacy benefit by dosing patients whose baseline platelet count of less than 2 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L}. The percentage of patients whose peak platelet count above 20 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} is shown in Figure 25.
L1196: **Figure 24:** Plot showing the percentage of patients falling within target platelet count after 7 daily doses of 3 000 µg or 4 000 µg lusutrombopag. BS_PLT represents baseline platelet count
L1197: ## Reviewer's Conclusions:
L1198: - Body weight was a significant covariate influencing the PK and PK/PD of lusutrombopag
- There is 10% increase in the response rate by raising the dose of lusutrombopag to 4-mg in patients weighting at least 100 kg,
- When dosed with 4-mg, the risk of having peak platelet count greater than 20 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} is minimal for patients weighting greater than 100 kg.
- Few patients with baseline count less than 2 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} are likely to benefit from lusutrombopag
L1199: In summary, this analysis showed that raising the dose to 4-mg in subjects with high body weight (\ge 100 \text{ kg}) may lead to about 10% increase in response rate, and the risk of having peak platelet count greater than 20 \times 10^4/\mu\text{L} could be minimal. However, this simulation assumed that the risk of thrombosis was only dependent on the platelet count. The inherent higher risk of thrombosis at over-weighted patients were not considered in the simulation.

L1225: ## Statistical Review and Evaluation
L1226: **NDA/BLA:** NDA210923
L1227: **Drug Name:** Lusutrombopag (S-888711)
L1228: **Indication:** for the treatment of thrombocytopenia in patients with chronic liver disease who are at increased risk for bleeding associated with elective invasive procedures
L1229: **Sponsor:** Shionogi
L1230: **Reviewer:** Jingjing Ye
L1231: **Date of Receipt:** Dec. 26, 2017
L1232: On Dec. 26, 2017, Shionogi submitted the results of their pivotal studies L-Plus1 and L-Plus2 in Lusutrombopag to support approval for the treatment of thrombocytopenia in patients with chronic liver disease who are at increased risk for bleeding associated with elective invasive procedures. This document briefly reviews the submitted material and conclusions made for the submission. More detailed information can be found in the unireview.
L1233: The pivotal trials L-Plus1 and L-Plus 2 were identical designs as a randomized, double-blind study to compare Lusutrombopag to placebo. Approximately 97 patients were enrolled in L-Plus 1 study and 215 patients were enrolled in L-Plus 2 study.
L1234: Analysis datasets, SDTM tabulations, and software codes are located at:
L1235: C:\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA210923\0002\m5\datasets\1304m0631
L1236: C:\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA210923\0002\m5\datasets\1423m0634
L1237: The primary efficacy endpoint was proportion of patients who require no platelet transfusion prior to the primary invasive procedure and no rescue therapy for bleeding from randomization through 7 days after the primary invasive procedure in L-Plus 2 and percentage of patients who required no platelet transfusion prior to invasive procedures in L-Plus 1 study. The studies L-Plus1 and L-Plus2 met the primary endpoint and key secondary endpoints comparing Lusutrombopag arm to placebo arm. There was statistically significant improvement in the proportion of patients not requiring platelet transfusion prior to invasive procedures in Lusutrombopag arm to placebo in L-plus 1 study and the difference of patients was 66% with the corresponding 95% confidence interval of 51% to 83%. There was also statistically significant improvement in the proportion of patients not requiring platelet transfusion prior to invasive procedure or rescue therapy for bleeding thorough 7 days after invasive procedure in Lusutrombopag arm to placebo in L-plus 2 study and the difference was 36% with corresponding 95% confidence interval of 24% to 49%.
L1238: For secondary endpoint of proportion of responders and duration of platelet count increase to at least 50 \times 10^9/L, the patients treated with Lusutrombopag showed statistically significant improvement over placebo arm in both L-Plus 1 and L-Plus 2 studies.
L1239: In conclusion, the L-Plus1 and L-Plus2 showed superiority of primary endpoint and secondary endpoints in Lusutrombopag arm compared to placebo arm.

L1255: ## 1. Background
L1256: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to assess the carcinogenic potential of S-888711 when administered orally by gavage to rats and mice for 104 weeks.
L1257: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L1258: ## 2. Rat Study
L1259: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated in Table 1, in each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred twenty five Crl:CD (SD) rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 65 rats per group. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 2, 6, and 20 mg/kg/day for male rats, and 0.5, 1, and 2 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively. In this review these dose groups were referred to as the low (Group 3), mid (Group 4), and high (Group 5) dose groups, respectively. The rats in the water control group and the vehicle control groups were administrated with water and the vehicle [100% Polyethylene glycol 400], and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.
L1260: **Table 1: Experimental Design in Rat Study**
L1261: Group No.	No. of Animals	Test Material	Dosage Level (mg/kg/day)
Male	Female	Male	Female
1	65	65	Water Control	0	0
2	65	65	Vehicle Control	0	0
3	65	65	S-888711 Low	2	0.5
4	65	65	S-888711 Mid	6	1
5	65	65	S-888711 High	20	2
L1262: Animals were inspected visually at least twice daily for evidence of ill-health or reaction to treatment. Daily during the first week of treatment, twice weekly during Weeks 2 to 4 (middle and end of each week), weekly during Weeks 5 to 13, once every two weeks during Weeks 14 to 52 and once every four weeks thereafter, detailed observations were recorded. In conjunction with the weekly physical examination, palpation was performed on all animals. Debilitated animals were observed carefully and, where necessary, isolated to prevent cannibalism. All animals were subject to a detailed necropsy. After a review of the history of each animal, a full macroscopic examination of the tissues was performed. All external features and orifices were examined visually.
L1263: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1264: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L1265: In the sponsor's analysis, the numbers of animal deaths during the study, up to terminal sacrifice, were analyzed by logrank tests for a trend across the groups. The numbers of animal deaths during the study were presented as life-tables and Kaplan-Meier survival curves. The following statistical tests were carried out:
L1266: 1) a two-tailed test for a trend with dose levels including Groups 2, 3, 4 and 5.
L1267: - a two-tailed pairwise comparison test of each treatment group against the vehicle control group.
- a two-tailed pairwise comparison test of the water control group against the vehicle control group.
L1268: Where the test for trend was statistically significant, the highest dose group was excluded and the trend test repeated (using a one-tailed test), until the test was no longer statistically significant. As a check, tests for non-linearity (not presented) were carried out. In this study, the non-linearity tests were not statistically significant at the 1% level and thus the results of the trend tests are to be preferred. Significance levels were calculated using \chi^2 tests and adjusted with a continuity correction where there was only one degree of freedom. These p-values have been quoted in the logrank results tables.
L1269: ## Sponsor's findings:
L1270: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (37%), 29 (45%), 26 (40%), 30 (46%), and 29 (45%) in Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 for male rats, respectively, and 29 (45%), 15 (38%), 20 (31%), 17 (27%), and 18 (28%) for female rats respectively. The trend test was not statistically significant when Groups 2, 3, 4 and 5 were included in the analysis. None of the pairwise comparisons were statistically significant for both male and female rats.
L1271: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1272: In the sponsor's analysis, all animals that died after terminal sacrifice commenced (Week 105) were considered terminal and the tumors observed in these animals were categorized as incidental. For incidental tumors, the following fixed time intervals were used to adjust for differential mortality between the treatment groups: 1-52, 53-78, 79-92, 93-104 weeks and terminal. Time-to-tumor log-rank analysis Log-rank methods were used to analyze the number of animals with tumors across treatment groups (Peto et al 1980). The log-rank analysis results tables show the total observed and expected number of tumors and the relative tumor rate across all time intervals. The observed and the expected frequencies were calculated for each time interval under a hypothesis of no difference between treatment groups. The observed and expected frequencies were then compared across the treatment groups. The following \chi^2 statistical tests were carried out:
L1273: - a one-tailed test for a trend using dose levels including Groups 2, 3, 4 and 5
- a one-tailed pairwise comparison test of each treatment group against the vehicle control group
- a two-tailed pairwise comparison test of the water control group against the vehicle control group
L1274: Where the test for trend was statistically significant, the highest dose group was excluded and the trend test was repeated, using a one-tailed test until the test was no longer statistically significant. The significance levels were adjusted using a continuity correction where there was one degree of freedom. As a check, tests for non-linearity were carried out (not presented). For benign haemangioma (mesenteric lymph node) in males the non-linearity test was significant (p=0.005), therefore the pairwise tests are to be preferred to the trend test. For all other analyses the non-linearity tests were not statistically significant at the 1% level, hence the trend tests are to be preferred.
L1275: Where there were fewer than ten observed tumors, exact one-tailed p-values were calculated using permutation tests for stratified contingency tables, to test for trend and for pairwise comparisons of each treatment group against the control group (SAS Institute Inc., 2002). All p-values are quoted in the time-to-tumor tables of results.
L1276: ## Adjustment for multiple testing:
L1277: In the sponsor's report, the rules of summarizing the results described in Lin (Lin, 2000) were adopted. These rules require an initial designation of tumor-types into "common" or "rare".
L1278: - Tumors with an historical frequency greater than 1%, were designated as being "common". A significance level of 0.005 was used for the trend tests, and 0.01 for each pairwise test.
- Tumors with an historical frequency less than or equal to 1% were designated as being "rare". A significance level of 0.025 was used for the trend tests, and 0.05 for each pairwise test.
L1279: The classification of tumors was carried out based upon the overall incidence in historical control data. The following list of statistically analyzed tumors was considered to be "rare" tumors, all other tumors in this report were considered to be "common" tumors.

L1286: ## 2.2. Reviewer's analyses
L1287: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
L1288: ## 2.2.1. Survival analysis
L1289: In the reviewer's analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all five groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 2, 3, 4, and 5 using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for all five groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data of all five groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for Groups 2, 3, 4, and 5 are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1290: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1291: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (37%), 29 (45%), 26 (40%), 30 (46%), and 29 (45%) in Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 for male rats, respectively, and 29 (45%), 15 (38%), 20 (31%), 17 (27%), and 18 (28%) for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats.
L1292: *Comment: There are 8 male rats in the study that died naturally but without information for their death time. These animals are #133 in vehicle control group; #196 in the low dose group, #277 in the high dose group, and #10, #16, #18, #35, and #42 in the water control group; whereas there are 2 female rats that died naturally but without information for their death time. These animals are #659 in the high dose group, and #341 in the water control group.*
L1293: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L1294: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across Groups 2, 3, 4, and 5, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups (Groups 3, 4, and 5) against the vehicle control group (Group 2), using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).
L1295: In the poly-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R_i is defined as R_i = \sum w_{ij} where w_{ij} is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the group.
L1296: Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w_{ij} as follows:
L1297: w_{ij} = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
L1298: w_{ij} = (t_{ij} / tsacr)^3 to animals dying without the tumor,
L1299: where t_{ij} is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and tsacr is the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be
L1300: assigned w_{ij} = 1 since t_{ij} = t_{sacr}. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the end of the study will be assigned as w_{ij} = 1.
L1301: Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the t_{sacr} should not be affected by the unplanned early terminations. The t_{sacr} should always be equal to the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than t_{sacr}, regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, t_{sacr} was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in the analysis.
L1302: One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data.
L1303: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L1304: For the adjustment of multiple testing, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the FDA guidance for statistical design and analysis of carcinogenicity studies (2001). For dose response relationship tests, the guidance suggests the use of test levels of \alpha=0.005 for common tumors and \alpha=0.025 for rare tumors for a submission with two species where both are two-years studies, in order to keep the false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%. For multiple pairwise comparisons of treated group with control, the guidance suggests the use of test levels of \alpha=0.01 for common tumors and \alpha=0.05 for rare tumors, in order to keep the false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10% for both submissions with two or one species.
L1305: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1306: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The tumor types with p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for dose response relationship and/or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and vehicle control are reported in Table 2.
L1307: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, the reviewer's analysis showed statistically significant increases for the incidence of haemangioma in ln mesenteric in male rats when comparing the mid dose group to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0084), and the incidence of combined endometrial adenoma and polyp in uterus in female rats when comparing the low dose group to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0099), regardless the common or rare classification of these tumors. No corresponding statistically significant dose response relationship was noted for these tumors.
L1308: No other statistically significant findings were noted in tumor data for both male and female rats.

L1315: ## 3. Mouse Study
L1316: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As indicated in Table 3, in each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred Crl:CD1(ICR) mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 mice per group. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 2, 6, and 20 mg/kg/day for both male and female mice. In this review these dose groups were referred to as the low (Group 3), mid (Group 4), and high (Group 5) dose groups, respectively. The mice in the water control group and the vehicle control group were administrated with water and the vehicle [100% Polyethylene glycol 400], and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.
L1317: Animals were inspected visually at least twice daily for evidence of ill-health or reaction to treatment. Daily during the first week of treatment, twice weekly during Weeks 2 to 4 (middle and end of each week), weekly during Weeks 5 to 13, once every two weeks during Weeks 14 to 52 and once every four weeks thereafter, detailed observations were recorded. In conjunction with the weekly physical examination, palpation was performed on all animals. Debilitated animals were observed carefully and, where necessary, isolated to prevent cannibalism. All
L1318: animals were subject to a detailed necropsy. After a review of the history of each animal, a full macroscopic examination of the tissues was performed. All external features and orifices were examined visually.

There were no statistically significant findings in mortality for both male or female mice.

L1350: ## 4. Summary
L1351: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to assess the carcinogenic potential of S-888711 when administered orally by gavage to rats and mice for 104 weeks.
L1352: ## Rat Study:
L1353: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred twenty five Crl:CD (SD) rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 65 rats per group. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 2, 6, and 20 mg/kg/day for male rats, and 0.5, 1, and 2 mg/kg/day for female rats, respectively.
L1354: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (37%), 29 (45%), 26 (40%), 30 (46%), and 29 (45%) in Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 for male rats, respectively, and 29 (45%), 15 (38%), 20 (31%), 17 (27%), and 18 (28%) for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats.
L1355: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, the reviewer's analysis showed statistically significant increases for the incidence of haemangioma in In mesenteric in male rats when comparing the mid dose group to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0084), and the incidence of combined endometrial adenoma and polyp in uterus in female rats when comparing the low dose group to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0099), regardless the common or rare classification of these tumors. No corresponding statistically significant dose response relationship was noted for these tumors. No other statistically significant findings were noted in tumor data for both male and female rats.
L1356: ## Mouse Study:
L1357: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred Crl:CD1(ICR) mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 mice per group. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 2, 6, and 20 mg/kg/day for both male and female mice.
L1358: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 33 (55%), 31 (52%), 34 (57%), 27 (45%), and 34 (57%) in Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 for male mice, respectively, and 22 (37%), 27 (45%), 28 (47%), 26 (43%), and 20 (33%) for female mice respectively. No statistically significant findings in mortality were noted for both male and female mice.
L1359: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, for male mice, the reviewer's analysis showed a statistically significant increase for the incidence of subcapsular cell adenoma in adrenals in the high dose group when compared to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0092), regardless whether the tumor is considered to be rare or common. No
L1360: corresponding statistically significant dose response relationship was noted for this tumor. No other statistically significant findings were noted in tumor data for both male and female mice.

The X-axis is DOSE (mg) with values 0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, and 2. The Y-axis is Proportion of Patients (%) from 0 to 90. The red line (Transfusion) starts high (around 80% at 0.5 mg) and decreases, crossing the blue line (Responders) around 1.75 mg. The blue line (Responders) starts at 0% and increases sharply after 1.5 mg, reaching about 35% at 2 mg.

The plot shows that C_{max} generally decreases as body weight increases, particularly in the highest weight group (<45kg) and the highest weight group (\ge 100kg).

L1448: Box plot illustrating the effect of body weight on population PK predicted AUC (ng*hr/mL). The Y-axis represents AUC (ng*hr/mL) ranging from 0 to 10000. The X-axis represents Body weight categories: <45kg (N=18), 45-<60kg (N=115), 60-<80kg (N=163), 80-<100 (N=44), and \ge100kg (N=9). The plot shows that AUC generally increases with body weight, with the highest median AUC observed in the 45-<60kg group.
L1449: Figure 5 is a box plot illustrating the effect of mild and moderate hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of lusutrombopag. The plot is divided into two rows: AUCinf (ng*h/mL) on the top and Cmax (ng/mL) on the bottom. The x-axis shows three groups: HEALTHY, MILD, and MODERATE hepatic impairment.
For AUCinf, the values are highest in the HEALTHY group (median around 350 ng*h/mL), followed by MILD (median around 380 ng*h/mL), and MODERATE (median around 450 ng*h/mL). The range of AUCinf values is generally higher in the MODERATE group compared to HEALTHY and MILD.
For Cmax, the values are highest in the HEALTHY group (median around 15 ng/mL), followed by MILD (median around 17 ng/mL), and MODERATE (median around 18 ng/mL). The range of Cmax values is generally higher in the MODERATE group compared to HEALTHY and MILD.
L1450: Figure 6 displays two scatter plots showing lusutrombopag pharmacokinetic parameters among patients with Child-Pugh Class A, B, and C liver disease. The left plot shows Cmax (ng/mL) and the right plot shows AUC0-t (ng*hr/mL). Both plots show individual values (circles) and mean (SD) values (triangles).
For Cmax (left plot):
Child-Pugh A: Mean Cmax is approximately 200 ng/mL.
Child-Pugh B: Mean Cmax is approximately 180 ng/mL.
Child-Pugh C: Mean Cmax is approximately 150 ng/mL.
For AUC0-t (right plot):
Child-Pugh A: Mean AUC0-t is approximately 4000 ng*hr/mL.
Child-Pugh B: Mean AUC0-t is approximately 3800 ng*hr/mL.
Child-Pugh C: Mean AUC0-t is approximately 3500 ng*hr/mL.
The data indicates a decrease in both Cmax and AUC0-t as the severity of hepatic impairment increases.
L1451: Scatter plot showing the relationship between creatinine clearance (CLcr, mL/min) on the x-axis (ranging from 50 to 250) and clearance estimates of lusutrombopag (CL/F, L/hr) on the y-axis (ranging from 0.5 to 2.0). A positive linear trend is indicated by a red line.
L1452: Figure 8: Box plot showing the effect of a high-fat, high-calorie meal on the relative bioavailability of lusutrombopag. The left panel shows AUC (ng*hr/mL) and the right panel shows Cmax (ng/mL). Both panels compare Fasted (white box) and Fed (black box) states. For AUC, the median decreases from approximately 4800 ng*hr/mL (Fasted) to 4400 ng*hr/mL (Fed). For Cmax, the median decreases from approximately 165 ng/mL (Fasted) to 155 ng/mL (Fed).
L1453: Figure 9 displays box plots illustrating the effect of cyclosporine co-administration on the exposure of lusutrombopag. The figure is divided into two main panels, each showing two metrics: AUCinf (ng*h/mL) and Cmax (ng/mL).
The left panel compares S-888711 + cyclosporine versus S-888711 alone. The right panel compares S-888711 + cyclosporine versus S-888711 alone.
For AUCinf (left panel): S-888711 + cyclosporine shows a significantly higher median AUCinf (around 4200 ng*h/mL) compared to S-888711 alone (around 3300 ng*h/mL). The difference is statistically significant (indicated by **).
For Cmax (right panel): S-888711 + cyclosporine shows a significantly higher median Cmax (around 140 ng/mL) compared to S-888711 alone (around 120 ng/mL). The difference is statistically significant (indicated by **).
L1454: Figure 10 displays box plots illustrating the effect of 4 g of sodium carbonate on the exposure of lusutrombopag. The figure is divided into two main panels, each showing two metrics: AUC (ng*hr/mL) and Cmax (ng/mL).
The left panel compares Fasted versus Fasted with calcium. The right panel compares Fasted versus Fasted with calcium.
For AUC (left panel): Fasted shows a significantly higher median AUC (around 680 ng*hr/mL) compared to Fasted with calcium (around 580 ng*hr/mL). The difference is statistically significant (indicated by **).
For Cmax (right panel): Fasted shows a significantly higher median Cmax (around 22 ng/mL) compared to Fasted with calcium (around 18 ng/mL). The difference is statistically significant (indicated by **).
L1455: Study Schematic of L-PLUS1 Study
The study timeline is divided into three periods: Screening period (1 to 28 days), Treatment Period (7 days), and Post-treatment Period (28 days).
Participants are randomized into two groups:
3-mg group: Once daily 3 mg/day
Placebo group: Once daily placebo
Key events:
Informed consent (Start of Screening period)
Registration (randomization) and initial study treatment (Day 1) (Start of Treatment Period)
Planned invasive procedure (Occurs Within 6 days of Day 1)
Day 9 to 14 (End of Treatment Period)
Final post-treatment day (Day 35) (End of Post-treatment Period)

L1458: Figure 14: L-PLUS2 Study Schematic
The study timeline is divided into three periods:
Screening Period (Up to 28 days): Includes Informed consent and Randomisation and initial dose of study drug (Day 1).
Treatment Period (7 days): Participants receive either S-888711 3 mg once daily or Placebo once daily, both up to 7 days.
Post-treatment Period (28 days): Includes an Invasive procedure (Days 9 to 14) and the Final study day (Day 35).

L1463: Figure 16 displays three panels showing the dose-normalized Visual Predictive Check (VPC) for the final pharmacokinetic model, comparing observed data (open circles) with model predictions (solid line for median, dashed lines for 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles). The dark grey shaded area represents the model predicted 95% CI of the median, and the grey shaded area represents the model predicted 95% CIs of the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles. The data is based on 200 replications.
The Y-axis for all panels is 3-mg normalized concentration (ng/mL), and the X-axis is Time (hr).
Top Panel: Single dose in healthy volunteers. The concentration decreases rapidly after the initial peak (around 50 ng/mL at 1 hr).
Middle Panel: Multiple doses in healthy volunteers. The concentration shows a fluctuating pattern with peaks around 100 ng/mL.
Bottom Panel: Multiple doses in patients. The concentration shows a fluctuating pattern with peaks around 200 ng/mL.
Figure 16: Dose-normalized Visual Predictive Check for the Final Pharmacokinetic Model for single-dose in healthy volunteers (top panel), multiple doses in healthy volunteers (middle panel), and multiple doses in patients (bottom panel). Open circle: observed data. Solid line: observed median. Dashed line: observed 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles. Dark grey shaded area: model predicted 95% CI of median. Grey shaded area: model predicted 95% CIs of 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles. 200 replications (Source: Applicant's population PK/PD report, Page 62-68, Figure 3)
L1464: Figure 17 displays two boxplots illustrating the effect of body weight on lusutrombopag exposure parameters (Cmax and AUC) following a 3-mg dose once daily for 7 days. The body weight groups are categorized as <45 kg (N=18), 45-<60 kg (N=115), 60-<80 kg (N=163), 80-<100 kg (N=44), and ≥100 kg (N=9).
The left boxplot shows Cmax (ng/mL). Cmax decreases as body weight increases, with the highest median Cmax observed in the <45 kg group (around 280 ng/mL) and the lowest median Cmax observed in the ≥100 kg group (around 100 ng/mL).
The right boxplot shows AUC (ng*hr/mL). AUC also decreases as body weight increases, with the highest median AUC observed in the <45 kg group (around 6500 ng*hr/mL) and the lowest median AUC observed in the ≥100 kg group (around 2500 ng*hr/mL).
Figure 17: Boxplots of Steady-state Cmax and AUC0-t in Patients by Body Weight Groups Estimated Using Post-hoc Pharmacokinetic Parameters following 3-mg Doses Once Daily for 7 Days (Source: Applicant's population PK/PD report, Page 66, Figure 10)
L1465: Figure 18 displays the Scheme of the Final Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Model for Lusutrombopag, divided into a Pharmacokinetic model (top) and a Pharmacodynamic model (bottom).
Pharmacokinetic model (Top): Comp1 (absorption compartment) is connected to Comp2 (central compartment) via the first-order rate constant KA. Comp2 is connected to Comp3 (peripheral compartment) via K_{23} and K_{32}. Comp2 is connected to Comp4 (peripheral compartment) via K_{24} and K_{42}. Comp2 is also connected to elimination via KE.
Pharmacodynamic model (Bottom): The model starts with K_{PR} (zero-order rate for the production of platelet precursor) leading to P1. P1 matures to P2 via KM (first-order rate constant for the maturation of platelet). P2 matures to P3 via KM. P3 matures to P4 via KM. P4 matures to PLT (platelet compartment) via KM. PLT is eliminated via KL (first-order rate constant for the elimination of platelet).
A dashed line labeled "Stimulant effect for platelet production" connects the Pharmacokinetic model (Comp2) to the Pharmacodynamic model (P1).
Figure 18: Scheme of Final Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Model for Lusutrombopag. Comp1: absorption compartment. Comp2: central compartment. Comp3 and Comp4: peripheral compartment. P1, P2, P3, and P4: hypothetical platelet precursor compartment. PLT: platelet compartment. KA: first-order rate constant of absorption. K_{23}, K_{32}, K_{24}, and K_{42}: first-order transfer rate constant between compartments. KE: first-order rate constant of elimination. C: plasma lusutrombopag concentration. K_{PR}: zero-order rate for the production of platelet precursor. KM: first-order rate constant for the maturation of platelet. KL: first-order rate constant for the elimination of platelet (Source: Applicant's population PK/PD report, Page 104, Appendix 12)
L1466: Figure 19: Prediction-corrected Visual Predictive Check for the Final Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Model for Patients. Upper figure: Linear scale. Lower figure:
The figure displays two prediction-corrected visual predictive checks (VPCs) for the final pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic model for patients. Both plots show Prediction-corrected platelet count (\times 10^4/\mu\text{L}) on the Y-axis versus Time (hr) on the X-axis (0 to 2500 hr).
The upper figure uses a linear scale for the Y-axis (0 to 15). The lower figure uses a logarithmic scale for the Y-axis (1 to 10).
In both plots, observed data points (blue dots) are scattered around the predicted median (red line) and the 95% prediction intervals (gray shaded area and dashed red lines). The observed data generally falls within the predicted intervals, indicating a good fit of the model to the data.

The plots show observed data (blue circles) against predicted values or time, along with a black solid line (line of identity or zero line) and a red solid line (trend line).
Top Left: Individual predictions versus concentrations. The x-axis is Individual predicted concentration (0 to 2500) and the y-axis is Observed concentration (0 to 2500). The data points generally follow the line of identity.
Top Right: Population predictions versus concentrations. The x-axis is Population predicted concentration (0 to 2500) and the y-axis is Observed concentration (0 to 2500). The data points generally follow the line of identity.
Bottom Left: Conditional weighted residuals versus population predictions. The x-axis is Population predicted concentration (0 to 2000) and the y-axis is CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals, -5 to 2). The residuals are scattered around the zero line (black dashed line), with a slight positive trend indicated by the red solid line.
Bottom Right: Conditional weighted residuals versus time. The x-axis is Time (hours) (0 to 1000) and the y-axis is CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals, -5 to 2). The residuals are scattered around the zero line (black dashed line), showing a slight positive trend indicated by the red solid line.

L1469: Figure 22 is a box and whiskers plot illustrating Lusutrombopag AUC (ng*hr/mL) at day 7 against Body weight (kg) using Phase 3 data. The Y-axis represents Lusutrombopag AUC (ng*hr/mL), ranging from 2000 to 8000. The X-axis represents Body weight (kg) in six categories: (<45 kg), (45 to <60 kg), (60 to <80 kg), (80 to <100 kg), (>=100 kg), and (>=100 kg, 4 mg). The plot shows that AUC generally increases with body weight. Red triangles indicate the AUC of patients who developed drug related thrombosis, typically falling within the upper quartile of the corresponding weight category. The last category, (>=100 kg, 4 mg), was specifically created to show the resulting AUC in patients weighting more than 100 kg and dosed with 4-mg lusutrombopag.
Figure 22: Box and whiskers plot for lusutrombopag AUC at day 7 against body weight using Phase 3 data. The last category ">=100 (4mg)" was created to show the resulting AUC in patients weighting at least 100 kg and dosed with 4-mg lusutrombopag. The red triangles represent the AUC of patients who developed drug related thrombosis
L1470: Figure 23 displays two panels of data related to platelet count response to lusutrombopag dosing (3000 µg or 4000 µg) after 7 daily doses, stratified by body weight (kg).
The left panel is a line graph showing the Percentage of patients WITHIN target (Y-axis, 0 to 100%) versus Body weight (kg) (X-axis, 60 to 150 kg). Two lines represent the 3000 µg dose (red circles) and the 4000 µg dose (teal circles). Both lines show a decreasing trend in the percentage of patients within target as body weight increases, with the 4000 µg dose consistently showing a higher percentage of patients within target than the 3000 µg dose across the weight range shown.
The right panel is a bar chart showing the Percentage of patients > 200,000/\mu\text{L} P.L.T. count (%) (Y-axis, 0 to 5%) versus Body weight (kg) (X-axis, categorized as <45 kg, 45 to <60 kg, 60 to <80 kg, 80 to <100 kg, and \ge 100 kg). Two bars represent the 3000 µg dose (red) and the 4000 µg dose (teal). The 4000 µg dose consistently shows a higher percentage of patients achieving a platelet count above 200,000/\mu\text{L} across all weight categories, particularly in the <45 kg and 45 to <60 kg categories.
Figure 23: Plot showing the percentage of patients falling within target platelet count after 7 daily doses of 3 000 µg or 4 000 µg lusutrombopag (left panel). The right panel shows the percentage of patients above the target.
L1471: Figure 24 displays five side-by-side line graphs showing the Percentage of patients within target (Y-axis, 0 to 100%) versus Body weight (kg) (X-axis, 60 to 150 kg). The graphs are stratified by Baseline Platelet (BS_PLT) count: 10,000/\mu\text{L}, 20,000/\mu\text{L}, 30,000/\mu\text{L}, 40,000/\mu\text{L}, and 49,000/\mu\text{L}. Two lines represent the 3000 µg dose (red circles) and the 4000 µg dose (teal circles).
For BS_PLT = 10,000/\mu\text{L} and BS_PLT = 20,000/\mu\text{L}, the percentage of patients within target is very low (near 0%) across all body weights, regardless of dose. For BS_PLT = 30,000/\mu\text{L}, the percentage of patients within target increases significantly with body weight, and the 4000 µg dose shows a higher percentage than the 3000 µg dose. For BS_PLT = 40,000/\mu\text{L} and BS_PLT = 49,000/\mu\text{L}, the percentage of patients within target is high (near 100%) across all body weights, with minimal difference between the 3000 µg and 4000 µg doses.
factor(DOSE) → 3000 → 4000
